I, um… I came in because I can’t sleep right anymore. I work overnight, so I know my schedule is already weird—people always say that, like “well of course you’re tired”—but it’s not like that. I get off my shift and I’m exhausted, but then I just lie there wide awake.

And then when I finally do fall asleep, I wake up scared, like jolted awake, and it feels like I didn’t actually rest at all. My whole day gets messed up after that.

I get off my shift and I’m exhausted, but then I just lie there wide awake.\n\nAnd then when I finally do fall asleep, I wake up scared, like jolted awake, and it feels like I didn’t actually rest at all. My whole day gets messed up after that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have bad dreams or nightmares when you wake up scared?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know if it’s dreams exactly. Sometimes I can’t remember anything, I just wake up scared, like my body thinks something happened. And then I’m just… up. I’ll be exhausted after my overnight shift, and still I’m lying there wide awake, then if I do drift off, it’s broken up and I don’t feel rested at all.\n\nIt messes up the whole day, because then I’m supposed to do normal stuff, and everyone else is awake and moving around, and I’m just stuck feeling like I never slept.\n\nDoctor: Did something specific happen that made you start experiencing these sleep problems?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, sort of. I don’t know if it was one specific thing like something dramatic happened, but it started after my agency changed my schedule. They put me on these rotating overnight shifts, and then there was extra paperwork too, like more than before.\n\nIt’s been… about a year and two months now, I think. Around then is when the sleep stuff really started getting bad. I remember because I kept thinking I’d adjust after a few weeks, like when you get used to a new bus route or something, but I just… didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge at times when you're not trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I guess everybody gets on edge sometimes, right? During the day it’s hard to tell, because I’m usually just trying to get through stuff, and then I start thinking about what I still have to do, like laundry or groceries, and then I forget why I walked into the kitchen… sorry, what was the question again?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’m not really sure. Like, if I’m doing dishes or walking to the store, I don’t think, “oh, I’m anxious,” you know? I guess… yeah, sometimes. Like I don’t want to do anything after work because I know if I get too stirred up, or even if I just try to lie down too soon, it gets messed up anyway. And then when I finally do fall asleep, it’s not even like real sleep. I wake up scared sometimes, like my body forgot I was supposed to be resting. So I end up avoiding things mostly because I’m worried I’ll feel even worse after, like more broken down. Does that make sense?

Mostly it’s after my overnight shifts. I don’t know if it’s a place exactly, but I start avoiding anything that might keep me up more, because I’ll come home exhausted and still just lie there wide awake.

I don’t know if it was one specific thing like something dramatic happened, but it started after my agency changed my schedule. I mostly just notice I’m distracted and worn out, not like a clear feeling I can name.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain activities or places because of how they might affect your sleep or how you feel afterwards?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, sometimes. Like I don’t want to do anything after work because I know if I get too stirred up, or even if I just try to lie down too soon, it gets messed up anyway. I’ll get off an overnight shift and I’m exhausted, but then I’m just laying there wide awake, staring at stuff, hearing the fridge or whatever.\n\nAnd then when I finally do fall asleep, it’s not even like real sleep. I wake up scared sometimes, like my body forgot I was supposed to be resting. So I end up avoiding things mostly because I’m worried I’ll feel even worse after, like more broken down. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there specific places or situations you avoid because they might trigger or worsen your sleep problems?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s after my overnight shifts. I don’t know if it’s a place exactly, but I start avoiding anything that might keep me up more, because I’ll come home exhausted and still just lie there wide awake.\n\nThen if I finally drift off, I wake up scared, like I didn’t actually rest. It’s like my sleep is all chopped up now. I came in because of that, really. I keep thinking maybe if I just do the right thing or stay away from certain stuff it’ll fix it, but… it doesn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever relive any frightening or unpleasant experiences from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not like a movie playing in my head or anything. I don’t really sit there and relive something specific, I don’t think.\n\nSometimes my mind goes off to old stuff, but then it’s like I’m thinking about work, or whether I paid something, or some random thing someone said to me years ago that probably didn’t even matter. It gets all mixed up, so I’m not sure what counts as “reliving.”\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid certain social situations or activities because you fear they might trigger anxiety or worsen your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… sort of. I don’t know if it’s social stuff exactly, like I’m not sitting there making a list of places I can’t go. It’s more that I just don’t want to do anything because I’m worried it’ll mess me up worse.\n\nI came in because my sleep is all broken up now. I get off an overnight shift and I should be dead tired, but I’ll just lie there wide awake. Then when I finally drift off, I wake up scared, and it feels like I never rested at all.\n\nSo if someone asks me to go somewhere after work, or do anything really, I start thinking, “If I do that, am I going to be even more wrecked?” And then I just… don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you feel like you're back in a frightening or unpleasant experience from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like I suddenly feel I’m back there, if that’s what you mean. I get stuck thinking about things sometimes, but then my mind jumps around, like one minute it’s old stuff and then it’s bills or work or something I forgot to do.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’ve had that “back in it” feeling. I just know my head won’t really settle.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Then when I finally drift off, I wake up scared, and it feels like I never rested at all.\n\nSo if someone asks me to go somewhere after work, or do anything really, I start thinking, “If I do that, am I going to be even more wrecked?” And then I just… don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you feel like you're back in a frightening or unpleasant experience from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like I suddenly feel I’m back there, if that’s what you mean. I just know my head won’t really settle.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving a frightening experience from your past, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I really get that, like reliving something. When you say it like that, it sounds like being pulled back into a moment, and I don’t know that that happens to me.\n\nI mean, I do get caught up in my head, but then it goes sideways—like I’ll think about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about work, or something I meant to buy, or some stupid thing somebody said. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF51.0, F43.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I, um… I came in because I can’t sleep right anymore. I work overnight, so I know my schedule is already weird—people always say that, like “well of course you’re tired”—but it’s not like that. I get off my shift and I’m exhausted, but then I just lie there wide awake.\n\nAnd then when I finally do fall asleep, I wake up scared, like jolted awake, and it feels like I didn’t actually rest at all. My whole day gets messed up after that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have bad dreams or nightmares when you wake up scared?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know if it’s dreams exactly. Sometimes I can’t remember anything, I just wake up scared, like my body thinks something happened. And then I’m just… up. I’ll be exhausted after my overnight shift, and still I’m lying there wide awake, then if I do drift off, it’s broken up and I don’t feel rested at all.\n\nIt messes up the whole day, because then I’m supposed to do normal stuff, and everyone else is awake and moving around, and I’m just stuck feeling like I never slept.\n\nDoctor: Did something specific happen that made you start experiencing these sleep problems?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, sort of. I don’t know if it was one specific thing like something dramatic happened, but it started after my agency changed my schedule. They put me on these rotating overnight shifts, and then there was extra paperwork too, like more than before.\n\nIt’s been… about a year and two months now, I think. Around then is when the sleep stuff really started getting bad. I remember because I kept thinking I’d adjust after a few weeks, like when you get used to a new bus route or something, but I just… didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge at times when you're not trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I guess everybody gets on edge sometimes, right? Like at work if somebody’s rushing me or if the bus is late, I get irritated, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nMostly I notice it around the sleep thing. During the day it’s hard to tell, because I’m usually just trying to get through stuff, and then I start thinking about what I still have to do, like laundry or groceries, and then I forget why I walked into the kitchen… sorry, what was the question again?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're doing everyday activities and not thinking about sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Like, if I’m doing dishes or walking to the store, I don’t think, “oh, I’m anxious,” you know? Sometimes I get jumpy or annoyed if there’s too much noise, but then I start thinking maybe I forgot something I needed to buy, and then I’m checking my pockets for my keys even though they’re in my hand.\n\nSo… I don’t know if that counts as anxious. I mostly just notice I’m distracted and worn out, not like a clear feeling I can name.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain activities or places because of how they might affect your sleep or how you feel afterwards?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, sometimes. Like I don’t want to do anything after work because I know if I get too stirred up, or even if I just try to lie down too soon, it gets messed up anyway. I’ll get off an overnight shift and I’m exhausted, but then I’m just laying there wide awake, staring at stuff, hearing the fridge or whatever.\n\nAnd then when I finally do fall asleep, it’s not even like real sleep. I wake up scared sometimes, like my body forgot I was supposed to be resting. So I end up avoiding things mostly because I’m worried I’ll feel even worse after, like more broken down. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there specific places or situations you avoid because they might trigger or worsen your sleep problems?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s after my overnight shifts. I don’t know if it’s a place exactly, but I start avoiding anything that might keep me up more, because I’ll come home exhausted and still just lie there wide awake.\n\nThen if I finally drift off, I wake up scared, like I didn’t actually rest. It’s like my sleep is all chopped up now. I came in because of that, really. I keep thinking maybe if I just do the right thing or stay away from certain stuff it’ll fix it, but… it doesn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever relive any frightening or unpleasant experiences from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not like a movie playing in my head or anything. I get off an overnight shift and I should be dead tired, but I’ll just lie there wide awake. I get stuck thinking about things sometimes, but then my mind jumps around, like one minute it’s old stuff and then it’s bills or work or something I forgot to do.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’ve had that “back in it” feeling. I just know my head won’t really settle.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving a frightening experience from your past, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I really get that, like reliving something. When you say it like that, it sounds like being pulled back into a moment, and I don’t know that that happens to me.\n\nI mean, I do get caught up in my head, but then it goes sideways—like I’ll think about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about work, or something I meant to buy, or some stupid thing somebody said. So I don’t know if that counts, but I wouldn’t say I feel like I’m back in some frightening thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're suddenly back in a moment from your past without actually being there?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I’m trying to think of what that would feel like. Like, I can remember stuff, sure, and sometimes my mind just wanders off and I’m thinking about something from years ago, and then somehow I’m thinking about what I need from the store or whether I left a light on, which is stupid because the electric bill’s already high enough…\n\nBut no, I don’t think I suddenly feel like I’m actually back there. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF51.0, F43.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I, um… I came in because I can’t sleep right anymore. I work overnight, so I know my schedule is already weird—people always say that, like “well of course you’re tired”—but it’s not like that. I get off my shift and I’m exhausted, but then I just lie there wide awake.\n\nAnd then when I finally do fall asleep, I wake up scared, like jolted awake, and it feels like I didn’t actually rest at all. My whole day gets messed up after that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have bad dreams or nightmares when you wake up scared?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know if it’s dreams exactly. Sometimes I can’t remember anything, I just wake up scared, like my body thinks something happened. And then I’m just… up. I’ll be exhausted after my overnight shift, and still I’m lying there wide awake, then if I do drift off, it’s broken up and I don’t feel rested at all.\n\nIt messes up the whole day, because then I’m supposed to do normal stuff, and everyone else is awake and moving around, and I’m just stuck feeling like I never slept.\n\nDoctor: Did something specific happen that made you start experiencing these sleep problems?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, sort of. I don’t know if it was one specific thing like something dramatic happened, but it started after my agency changed my schedule. They put me on these rotating overnight shifts, and then there was extra paperwork too, like more than before.\n\nIt’s been… about a year and two months now, I think. Around then is when the sleep stuff really started getting bad. I remember because I kept thinking I’d adjust after a few weeks, like when you get used to a new bus route or something, but I just… didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge at times when you're not trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I guess everybody gets on edge sometimes, right? Like at work if somebody’s rushing me or if the bus is late, I get irritated, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nMostly I notice it around the sleep thing. During the day it’s hard to tell, because I’m usually just trying to get through stuff, and then I start thinking about what I still have to do, like laundry or groceries, and then I forget why I walked into the kitchen… sorry, what was the question again?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're doing everyday activities and not thinking about sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Like, if I’m doing dishes or walking to the store, I don’t think, “oh, I’m anxious,” you know? Sometimes I get jumpy or annoyed if there’s too much noise, but then I start thinking maybe I forgot something I needed to buy, and then I’m checking my pockets for my keys even though they’re in my hand.\n\nSo… I don’t know if that counts as anxious. I mostly just notice I’m distracted and worn out, not like a clear feeling I can name.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain activities or places because of how they might affect your sleep or how you feel afterwards?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, sometimes. Like I don’t want to do anything after work because I know if I get too stirred up, or even if I just try to lie down too soon, it gets messed up anyway. I’ll get off an overnight shift and I’m exhausted, but then I’m just laying there wide awake, staring at stuff, hearing the fridge or whatever.\n\nAnd then when I finally do fall asleep, it’s not even like real sleep. I wake up scared sometimes, like my body forgot I was supposed to be resting. So I end up avoiding things mostly because I’m worried I’ll feel even worse after, like more broken down. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there specific places or situations you avoid because they might trigger or worsen your sleep problems?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s after my overnight shifts. I don’t know if it’s a place exactly, but I start avoiding anything that might keep me up more, because I’ll come home exhausted and still just lie there wide awake.\n\nThen if I finally drift off, I wake up scared, like I didn’t actually rest. It’s like my sleep is all chopped up now. I came in because of that, really. I keep thinking maybe if I just do the right thing or stay away from certain stuff it’ll fix it, but… it doesn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever relive any frightening or unpleasant experiences from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not like a movie playing in my head or anything. I don’t really sit there and relive something specific, I don’t think.\n\nSometimes my mind goes off to old stuff, but then it’s like I’m thinking about work, or whether I paid something, or some random thing someone said to me years ago that probably didn’t even matter. It gets all mixed up, so I’m not sure what counts as “reliving.”\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid certain social situations or activities because you fear they might trigger anxiety or worsen your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… sort of. It gets all mixed up, so I’m not sure what counts as “reliving.”\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid certain social situations or activities because you fear they might trigger anxiety or worsen your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… sort of. I don’t know if it’s social stuff exactly, like I’m not sitting there making a list of places I can’t go. It’s more that I just don’t want to do anything because I’m worried it’ll mess me up worse.\n\nI came in because my sleep is all broken up now. I get off an overnight shift and I should be dead tired, but I’ll just lie there wide awake. Then when I finally drift off, I wake up scared, and it feels like I never rested at all.\n\nSo if someone asks me to go somewhere after work, or do anything really, I start thinking, “If I do that, am I going to be even more wrecked?” And then I just… don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you feel like you're back in a frightening or unpleasant experience from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like I suddenly feel I’m back there, if that’s what you mean. I get stuck thinking about things sometimes, but then my mind jumps around, like one minute it’s old stuff and then it’s bills or work or something I forgot to do.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’ve had that “back in it” feeling. I just know my head won’t really settle.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving a frightening experience from your past, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I really get that, like reliving something. When you say it like that, it sounds like being pulled back into a moment, and I don’t know that that happens to me.\n\nI mean, I do get caught up in my head, but then it goes sideways—like I’ll think about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about work, or something I meant to buy, or some stupid thing somebody said. So I don’t know if that counts, but I wouldn’t say I feel like I’m back in some frightening thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're suddenly back in a moment from your past without actually being there?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I’m trying to think of what that would feel like. Like, I can remember stuff, sure, and sometimes my mind just wanders off and I’m thinking about something from years ago, and then somehow I’m thinking about what I need from the store or whether I left a light on, which is stupid because the electric bill’s already high enough…\n\nBut no, I don’t think I suddenly feel like I’m actually back there. It’s more just thoughts bouncing around, not like I’m in the moment again.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where it feels like you're reliving a past experience, even if it's not a complete memory?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so… not in the way you’re saying it. Like, I can have a memory pop up, but then I’m thinking about something totally different before I even finish the thought, like whether I fed the cat or if I’m late on something, and then I get annoyed because I can’t keep anything straight.\n\nBut feeling like I’m reliving it, even part of it… I don’t know. I haven’t noticed that. It’s more like my brain just drifts all over the place.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Sometimes I get jumpy or annoyed if there’s too much noise, but then I start thinking maybe I forgot something I needed to buy, and then I’m checking my pockets for my keys even though they’re in my hand.\n\nSo… I don’t know if that counts as anxious. I mostly just notice I’m distracted and worn out, not like a clear feeling I can name.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain activities or places because of how they might affect your sleep or how you feel afterwards?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, sometimes. Like I don’t want to do anything after work because I know if I get too stirred up, or even if I just try to lie down too soon, it gets messed up anyway. I’ll get off an overnight shift and I’m exhausted, but then I’m just laying there wide awake, staring at stuff, hearing the fridge or whatever.\n\nAnd then when I finally do fall asleep, it’s not even like real sleep. I wake up scared sometimes, like my body forgot I was supposed to be resting. So I end up avoiding things mostly because I’m worried I’ll feel even worse after, like more broken down. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there specific places or situations you avoid because they might trigger or worsen your sleep problems?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s after my overnight shifts. I don’t know if it’s a place exactly, but I start avoiding anything that might keep me up more, because I’ll come home exhausted and still just lie there wide awake.\n\nThen if I finally drift off, I wake up scared, like I didn’t actually rest. It’s like my sleep is all chopped up now. I came in because of that, really. I keep thinking maybe if I just do the right thing or stay away from certain stuff it’ll fix it, but… it doesn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever relive any frightening or unpleasant experiences from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not like a movie playing in my head or anything. I don’t really sit there and relive something specific, I don’t think.\n\nSometimes my mind goes off to old stuff, but then it’s like I’m thinking about work, or whether I paid something, or some random thing someone said to me years ago that probably didn’t even matter. It gets all mixed up, so I’m not sure what counts as “reliving.”\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid certain social situations or activities because you fear they might trigger anxiety or worsen your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… sort of. I don’t know if it’s social stuff exactly, like I’m not sitting there making a list of places I can’t go. It’s more that I just don’t want to do anything because I’m worried it’ll mess me up worse.\n\nI came in because my sleep is all broken up now. I get off an overnight shift and I should be dead tired, but I’ll just lie there wide awake. Then when I finally drift off, I wake up scared, and it feels like I never rested at all.\n\nSo if someone asks me to go somewhere after work, or do anything really, I start thinking, “If I do that, am I going to be even more wrecked?” And then I just… don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you feel like you're back in a frightening or unpleasant experience from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like I suddenly feel I’m back there, if that’s what you mean. I get stuck thinking about things sometimes, but then my mind jumps around, like one minute it’s old stuff and then it’s bills or work or something I forgot to do.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’ve had that “back in it” feeling. I just know my head won’t really settle.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving a frightening experience from your past, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I really get that, like reliving something. When you say it like that, it sounds like being pulled back into a moment, and I don’t know that that happens to me.\n\nI mean, I do get caught up in my head, but then it goes sideways—like I’ll think about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about work, or something I meant to buy, or some stupid thing somebody said. Like, I can remember stuff, sure, and sometimes my mind just wanders off and I’m thinking about something from years ago, and then somehow I’m thinking about what I need from the store or whether I left a light on, which is stupid because the electric bill’s already high enough…\n\nBut no, I don’t think I suddenly feel like I’m actually back there. It’s more just thoughts bouncing around, not like I’m in the moment again.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where it feels like you're reliving a past experience, even if it's not a complete memory?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so… not in the way you’re saying it. Like, I can have a memory pop up, but then I’m thinking about something totally different before I even finish the thought, like whether I fed the cat or if I’m late on something, and then I get annoyed because I can’t keep anything straight.\n\nBut feeling like I’m reliving it, even part of it… I don’t know. I haven’t noticed that. It’s more like my brain just drifts all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you feel like you're actually back in a scary or unpleasant moment from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I feel actually back there, no. Like I can think about something bad or uncomfortable, but it’s still just me sitting here thinking about it, not like I’m in it again.\n\nSometimes I get stuck on a thought and then I’m off on something else, like yesterday I was trying to remember if I paid my phone bill and then I ended up thinking about this lady I used to help at work who always wanted her towels folded a certain way… sorry, that’s not really what you asked. It’s like my sleep is all chopped up now. I came in because of that, really. I keep thinking maybe if I just do the right thing or stay away from certain stuff it’ll fix it, but… it doesn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever relive any frightening or unpleasant experiences from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not like a movie playing in my head or anything. I don’t really sit there and relive something specific, I don’t think.\n\nSometimes my mind goes off to old stuff, but then it’s like I’m thinking about work, or whether I paid something, or some random thing someone said to me years ago that probably didn’t even matter. It gets all mixed up, so I’m not sure what counts as “reliving.”\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid certain social situations or activities because you fear they might trigger anxiety or worsen your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… sort of. I don’t know if it’s social stuff exactly, like I’m not sitting there making a list of places I can’t go. It’s more that I just don’t want to do anything because I’m worried it’ll mess me up worse.\n\nI came in because my sleep is all broken up now. I get off an overnight shift and I should be dead tired, but I’ll just lie there wide awake. Then when I finally drift off, I wake up scared, and it feels like I never rested at all.\n\nSo if someone asks me to go somewhere after work, or do anything really, I start thinking, “If I do that, am I going to be even more wrecked?” And then I just… don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you feel like you're back in a frightening or unpleasant experience from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like I suddenly feel I’m back there, if that’s what you mean. I get stuck thinking about things sometimes, but then my mind jumps around, like one minute it’s old stuff and then it’s bills or work or something I forgot to do.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’ve had that “back in it” feeling. I just know my head won’t really settle.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving a frightening experience from your past, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I really get that, like reliving something. I haven’t noticed that. Like at work if somebody’s rushing me or if the bus is late, I get irritated, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nMostly I notice it around the sleep thing. During the day it’s hard to tell, because I’m usually just trying to get through stuff, and then I start thinking about what I still have to do, like laundry or groceries, and then I forget why I walked into the kitchen… sorry, what was the question again?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're doing everyday activities and not thinking about sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Like, if I’m doing dishes or walking to the store, I don’t think, “oh, I’m anxious,” you know? Sometimes I get jumpy or annoyed if there’s too much noise, but then I start thinking maybe I forgot something I needed to buy, and then I’m checking my pockets for my keys even though they’re in my hand.\n\nSo… I don’t know if that counts as anxious. I mostly just notice I’m distracted and worn out, not like a clear feeling I can name.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain activities or places because of how they might affect your sleep or how you feel afterwards?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, sometimes. Like I don’t want to do anything after work because I know if I get too stirred up, or even if I just try to lie down too soon, it gets messed up anyway. I’ll get off an overnight shift and I’m exhausted, but then I’m just laying there wide awake, staring at stuff, hearing the fridge or whatever.\n\nAnd then when I finally do fall asleep, it’s not even like real sleep. I wake up scared sometimes, like my body forgot I was supposed to be resting. So I end up avoiding things mostly because I’m worried I’ll feel even worse after, like more broken down. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there specific places or situations you avoid because they might trigger or worsen your sleep problems?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s after my overnight shifts. I don’t know if it’s a place exactly, but I start avoiding anything that might keep me up more, because I’ll come home exhausted and still just lie there wide awake.\n\nThen if I finally drift off, I wake up scared, like I didn’t actually rest. It’s like my sleep is all chopped up now. I came in because of that, really. I keep thinking maybe if I just do the right thing or stay away from certain stuff it’ll fix it, but… it doesn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever relive any frightening or unpleasant experiences from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not like a movie playing in my head or anything. I don’t really sit there and relive something specific, I don’t think.\n\nSometimes my mind goes off to old stuff, but then it’s like I’m thinking about work, or whether I paid something, or some random thing someone said to me years ago that probably didn’t even matter. It gets all mixed up, so I’m not sure what counts as “reliving.”\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid certain social situations or activities because you fear they might trigger anxiety or worsen your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… sort of. I don’t know if it’s social stuff exactly, like I’m not sitting there making a list of places I can’t go. It’s more that I just don’t want to do anything because I’m worried it’ll mess me up worse.\n\nI came in because my sleep is all broken up now. I get off an overnight shift and I should be dead tired, but I’ll just lie there wide awake. Then when I finally drift off, I wake up scared, and it feels like I never rested at all.\n\nSo if someone asks me to go somewhere after work, or do anything really, I start thinking, “If I do that, am I going to be even more wrecked?” And then I just… don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you feel like you're back in a frightening or unpleasant experience from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like I suddenly feel I’m back there, if that’s what you mean. I get stuck thinking about things sometimes, but then my mind jumps around, like one minute it’s old stuff and then it’s bills or work or something I forgot to do.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’ve had that “back in it” feeling. I just know my head won’t really settle.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving a frightening experience from your past, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I really get that, like reliving something. When you say it like that, it sounds like being pulled back into a moment, and I don’t know that that happens to me.\n\nI mean, I do get caught up in my head, but then it goes sideways—like I’ll think about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about work, or something I meant to buy, or some stupid thing somebody said. So I don’t know if that counts, but I wouldn’t say I feel like I’m back in some frightening thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're suddenly back in a moment from your past without actually being there?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I’m trying to think of what that would feel like. Like, I can remember stuff, sure, and sometimes my mind just wanders off and I’m thinking about something from years ago, and then somehow I’m thinking about what I need from the store or whether I left a light on, which is stupid because the electric bill’s already high enough…\n\nBut no, I don’t think I suddenly feel like I’m actually back there. It’s more just thoughts bouncing around, not like I’m in the moment again.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where it feels like you're reliving a past experience, even if it's not a complete memory?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so… not in the way you’re saying it. Like, I can have a memory pop up, but then I’m thinking about something totally different before I even finish the thought, like whether I fed the cat or if I’m late on something, and then I get annoyed because I can’t keep anything straight.\n\nBut feeling like I’m reliving it, even part of it… I don’t know. I haven’t noticed that. It’s more like my brain just drifts all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you feel like you're actually back in a scary or unpleasant moment from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I feel actually back there, no. Like I can think about something bad or uncomfortable, but it’s still just me sitting here thinking about it, not like I’m in it again.\n\nSometimes I get stuck on a thought and then I’m off on something else, like yesterday I was trying to remember if I paid my phone bill and then I ended up thinking about this lady I used to help at work who always wanted her towels folded a certain way… sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But no, I haven’t noticed feeling like I’m suddenly back in a scary moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're suddenly back in a moment from your past, reliving it as if it's happening now?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I know I already kind of said that, but no, not like it’s happening now. I can remember things, or I’ll start thinking about something from before, but then my brain jumps to something dumb like whether I washed my work pants or if there’s enough milk left, and then I lose the whole thread.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really felt like I’m suddenly back there reliving it. It’s more just remembering, I guess, and then getting distracted.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your life that might be connected to your sleep problems?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I know everybody has bad stuff that happens, but I don’t know if there’s one thing I’d call traumatic or that ties to my sleep.\n\nMy sleep gets messed up, but then I start thinking it’s because I stayed up too late, or the room feels wrong, or I forgot to do something—like last night I kept wondering if I left laundry in the washer, which I didn’t even do laundry, so that was stupid. But, um… I don’t know. I can’t point to some event and say that’s why.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced something very upsetting or disturbing that might be affecting your sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I’ve had upsetting things, sure, but I can’t think of something specific that’s, like, coming back at night and messing with my sleep.\n\nSometimes I start worrying about random stuff when I’m trying to sleep, like whether I forgot something at work or if I need to wash my sheets, and then I’ll remember some awkward thing from years ago, but I don’t know if that counts as disturbing. So… I’m not sure there’s one event like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I mean, I’ve had upsetting things, sure, but I can’t think of something specific that’s, like, coming back at night and messing with my sleep.\n\nSometimes I start worrying about random stuff when I’m trying to sleep, like whether I forgot something at work or if I need to wash my sheets, and then I’ll remember some awkward thing from years ago, but I don’t know if that counts as disturbing. So… I’m not sure there’s one event like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a traumatic experience that you think might be connected to your current sleep problems?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 26 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… I don’t think I have a clear answer for that. Like, nothing that I can point to and say, “That’s the thing causing my sleep to be bad.”

I’ve had hard stuff happen, but then when I try to think about it, I get kind of sidetracked—like I’ll remember I need to buy soap or that I never called someone back, and then I’m not even thinking about the original question anymore. So… I’m not sure. I haven’t noticed a specific traumatic thing connected to my sleep.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I, um… I came in because I can’t sleep right anymore. I work overnight, so I know my schedule is already weird—people always say that, like “well of course you’re tired”—but it’s not like that. I get off my shift and I’m exhausted, but then I just lie there wide awake.\n\nAnd then when I finally do fall asleep, I wake up scared, like jolted awake, and it feels like I didn’t actually rest at all. My whole day gets messed up after that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have bad dreams or nightmares when you wake up scared?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know if it’s dreams exactly. Sometimes I can’t remember anything, I just wake up scared, like my body thinks something happened. And then I’m just… up. I’ll be exhausted after my overnight shift, and still I’m lying there wide awake, then if I do drift off, it’s broken up and I don’t feel rested at all.\n\nIt messes up the whole day, because then I’m supposed to do normal stuff, and everyone else is awake and moving around, and I’m just stuck feeling like I never slept.\n\nDoctor: Did something specific happen that made you start experiencing these sleep problems?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, sort of. I don’t know if it was one specific thing like something dramatic happened, but it started after my agency changed my schedule. Around then is when the sleep stuff really started getting bad. I remember because I kept thinking I’d adjust after a few weeks, like when you get used to a new bus route or something, but I just… didn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or on edge at times when you're not trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. During the day it’s hard to tell, because I’m usually just trying to get through stuff, and then I start thinking about what I still have to do, like laundry or groceries, and then I forget why I walked into the kitchen… sorry, what was the question again?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're doing everyday activities and not thinking about sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Like, if I’m doing dishes or walking to the store, I don’t think, “oh, I’m anxious,” you know? Sometimes I get jumpy or annoyed if there’s too much noise, but then I start thinking maybe I forgot something I needed to buy, and then I’m checking my pockets for my keys even though they’re in my hand.\n\nSo… I don’t know if that counts as anxious. I mostly just notice I’m distracted and worn out, not like a clear feeling I can name.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain activities or places because of how they might affect your sleep or how you feel afterwards?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, sometimes. Like I don’t want to do anything after work because I know if I get too stirred up, or even if I just try to lie down too soon, it gets messed up anyway. I’ll get off an overnight shift and I’m exhausted, but then I’m just laying there wide awake, staring at stuff, hearing the fridge or whatever.\n\nAnd then when I finally do fall asleep, it’s not even like real sleep. I wake up scared sometimes, like my body forgot I was supposed to be resting. So I end up avoiding things mostly because I’m worried I’ll feel even worse after, like more broken down. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there specific places or situations you avoid because they might trigger or worsen your sleep problems?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s after my overnight shifts. I don’t know if it’s a place exactly, but I start avoiding anything that might keep me up more, because I’ll come home exhausted and still just lie there wide awake.\n\nThen if I finally drift off, I wake up scared, like I didn’t actually rest. It’s like my sleep is all chopped up now. I came in because of that, really. I keep thinking maybe if I just do the right thing or stay away from certain stuff it’ll fix it, but… it doesn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever relive any frightening or unpleasant experiences from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not like a movie playing in my head or anything. I don’t really sit there and relive something specific, I don’t think.\n\nSometimes my mind goes off to old stuff, but then it’s like I’m thinking about work, or whether I paid something, or some random thing someone said to me years ago that probably didn’t even matter. It gets all mixed up, so I’m not sure what counts as “reliving.”\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid certain social situations or activities because you fear they might trigger anxiety or worsen your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… sort of. I don’t know if it’s social stuff exactly, like I’m not sitting there making a list of places I can’t go. It’s more that I just don’t want to do anything because I’m worried it’ll mess me up worse.\n\nI came in because my sleep is all broken up now. I get off an overnight shift and I should be dead tired, but I’ll just lie there wide awake. Then when I finally drift off, I wake up scared, and it feels like I never rested at all.\n\nSo if someone asks me to go somewhere after work, or do anything really, I start thinking, “If I do that, am I going to be even more wrecked?” And then I just… don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you feel like you're back in a frightening or unpleasant experience from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like I suddenly feel I’m back there, if that’s what you mean. I get stuck thinking about things sometimes, but then my mind jumps around, like one minute it’s old stuff and then it’s bills or work or something I forgot to do.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’ve had that “back in it” feeling. I just know my head won’t really settle.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving a frightening experience from your past, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I really get that, like reliving something. When you say it like that, it sounds like being pulled back into a moment, and I don’t know that that happens to me.\n\nI mean, I do get caught up in my head, but then it goes sideways—like I’ll think about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about work, or something I meant to buy, or some stupid thing somebody said. So I don’t know if that counts, but I wouldn’t say I feel like I’m back in some frightening thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're suddenly back in a moment from your past without actually being there?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I’m trying to think of what that would feel like. Like, I can remember stuff, sure, and sometimes my mind just wanders off and I’m thinking about something from years ago, and then somehow I’m thinking about what I need from the store or whether I left a light on, which is stupid because the electric bill’s already high enough…\n\nBut no, I don’t think I suddenly feel like I’m actually back there. It’s more just thoughts bouncing around, not like I’m in the moment again.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where it feels like you're reliving a past experience, even if it's not a complete memory?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so… not in the way you’re saying it. Like, I can have a memory pop up, but then I’m thinking about something totally different before I even finish the thought, like whether I fed the cat or if I’m late on something, and then I get annoyed because I can’t keep anything straight.\n\nBut feeling like I’m reliving it, even part of it… I don’t know. I haven’t noticed that. It’s more like my brain just drifts all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you feel like you're actually back in a scary or unpleasant moment from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I feel actually back there, no. I wake up scared sometimes, like my body forgot I was supposed to be resting. So I end up avoiding things mostly because I’m worried I’ll feel even worse after, like more broken down. I don’t know if it’s a place exactly, but I start avoiding anything that might keep me up more, because I’ll come home exhausted and still just lie there wide awake.\n\nThen if I finally drift off, I wake up scared, like I didn’t actually rest. It’s like my sleep is all chopped up now. I came in because of that, really. I keep thinking maybe if I just do the right thing or stay away from certain stuff it’ll fix it, but… it doesn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever relive any frightening or unpleasant experiences from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not like a movie playing in my head or anything. I don’t really sit there and relive something specific, I don’t think.\n\nSometimes my mind goes off to old stuff, but then it’s like I’m thinking about work, or whether I paid something, or some random thing someone said to me years ago that probably didn’t even matter. It gets all mixed up, so I’m not sure what counts as “reliving.”\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid certain social situations or activities because you fear they might trigger anxiety or worsen your sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… sort of. I don’t know if it’s social stuff exactly, like I’m not sitting there making a list of places I can’t go. It’s more that I just don’t want to do anything because I’m worried it’ll mess me up worse.\n\nI came in because my sleep is all broken up now. I get off an overnight shift and I should be dead tired, but I’ll just lie there wide awake. Then when I finally drift off, I wake up scared, and it feels like I never rested at all.\n\nSo if someone asks me to go somewhere after work, or do anything really, I start thinking, “If I do that, am I going to be even more wrecked?” And then I just… don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you feel like you're back in a frightening or unpleasant experience from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like I suddenly feel I’m back there, if that’s what you mean. I get stuck thinking about things sometimes, but then my mind jumps around, like one minute it’s old stuff and then it’s bills or work or something I forgot to do.\n\nSo… I’m not sure I’ve had that “back in it” feeling. I just know my head won’t really settle.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving a frightening experience from your past, even if it's just for a moment?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I really get that, like reliving something. When you say it like that, it sounds like being pulled back into a moment, and I don’t know that that happens to me.\n\nI mean, I do get caught up in my head, but then it goes sideways—like I’ll think about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about work, or something I meant to buy, or some stupid thing somebody said. So I don’t know if that counts, but I wouldn’t say I feel like I’m back in some frightening thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're suddenly back in a moment from your past without actually being there?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I’m trying to think of what that would feel like. Like, I can remember stuff, sure, and sometimes my mind just wanders off and I’m thinking about something from years ago, and then somehow I’m thinking about what I need from the store or whether I left a light on, which is stupid because the electric bill’s already high enough…\n\nBut no, I don’t think I suddenly feel like I’m actually back there. I haven’t noticed that. It’s more like my brain just drifts all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you feel like you're actually back in a scary or unpleasant moment from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I feel actually back there, no. When you say it like that, it sounds like being pulled back into a moment, and I don’t know that that happens to me.\n\nI mean, I do get caught up in my head, but then it goes sideways—like I’ll think about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about work, or something I meant to buy, or some stupid thing somebody said. So I don’t know if that counts, but I wouldn’t say I feel like I’m back in some frightening thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're suddenly back in a moment from your past without actually being there?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I’m trying to think of what that would feel like. Like, I can remember stuff, sure, and sometimes my mind just wanders off and I’m thinking about something from years ago, and then somehow I’m thinking about what I need from the store or whether I left a light on, which is stupid because the electric bill’s already high enough…\n\nBut no, I don’t think I suddenly feel like I’m actually back there. It’s more just thoughts bouncing around, not like I’m in the moment again.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where it feels like you're reliving a past experience, even if it's not a complete memory?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so… not in the way you’re saying it. Like, I can have a memory pop up, but then I’m thinking about something totally different before I even finish the thought, like whether I fed the cat or if I’m late on something, and then I get annoyed because I can’t keep anything straight.\n\nBut feeling like I’m reliving it, even part of it… I don’t know. I haven’t noticed that. It’s more like my brain just drifts all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you feel like you're actually back in a scary or unpleasant moment from your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I feel actually back there, no. Like I can think about something bad or uncomfortable, but it’s still just me sitting here thinking about it, not like I’m in it again.\n\nSometimes I get stuck on a thought and then I’m off on something else, like yesterday I was trying to remember if I paid my phone bill and then I ended up thinking about this lady I used to help at work who always wanted her towels folded a certain way… sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But no, I haven’t noticed feeling like I’m suddenly back in a scary moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're suddenly back in a moment from your past, reliving it as if it's happening now?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I know I already kind of said that, but no, not like it’s happening now. I mean, I know everybody has bad stuff that happens, but I don’t know if there’s one thing I’d call traumatic or that ties to my sleep.\n\nMy sleep gets messed up, but then I start thinking it’s because I stayed up too late, or the room feels wrong, or I forgot to do something—like last night I kept wondering if I left laundry in the washer, which I didn’t even do laundry, so that was stupid. So… I’m not sure there’s one event like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a traumatic experience that you think might be connected to your current sleep problems?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think I have a clear answer for that. I haven’t noticed a specific traumatic thing connected to my sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced or witnessed something traumatic that still bothers you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think there’s anything like that that still bothers me in a clear way.
